## Context

- The most-copied code shape: thousands of builtins. A change after the freeze touches all of them [R07: Brimstone's handle refactor].
- Depends on: H10 (CSS keeps Rust locals alive), H14 (frame layout: `Args` reads the host frame), H06 (`Value` wraps `NonZeroU64`), H19 (atoms are the property keys), H22 (realms, R5). Depended on by: the builtins (P2, P3), H13 (BuiltinId index list), H35 (unit link cells), the JIT and AOT host-call stub.

## Decision

Plan row (02 §6): `fn(cx, this, args, new_target) -> JsResult<Local<'s>>`. `Args` is a frame view; `Throw` is a ZST; no `&mut` to a heap object across a JS call. AOT code reaches builtins through the isolate table or the link table. The row said `-> JsResult<Value>` until the v1 draft put `Local` in place of `Value` (a rewrite of the row's result type before the freeze; P4-DOCS updated 02 in 6bebbb1, M0 wk4):

```rust
pub type HostFn = for<'s> fn(&mut Cx<'s>, Local<'s>, Args<'s>, Local<'s>) -> JsResult<Local<'s>>;
//                           cx           this       args (= fp)  new_target
```

| Type | What it is | Where |
|---|---|---|
| `Cx<'s>` | the context: ObjectOps, conversions, calls, `throw_*`; exposes no `&Runtime` | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/mod.rs:509` |
| `Local<'s, T>` | the value itself, `repr(transparent)` over `Value`; `Copy`, `!Send`, `!Sync`; markers `JsValue` (default), `JsObject`, `JsFunction`, `JsArray`, `JsString`, `FlatStr`, `JsSymbol`, `JsKey`; checked downcasts, `From` upcasts | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/local.rs:147` |
| `Args<'s>` | one word, the host frame pointer: `len`, `get`, `this`, `new_target`, `callee`, `rest(i)` | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/mod.rs:100` |
| `PropertyDescriptor<'s>` | §6.2.6, every field optional | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/mod.rs:229` |
| `ScratchList<'s>` | LIFO precise list over `ep9_heap::ScratchRoots` | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/mod.rs:378` |
| `ArgsBuilder<'s>` | call arguments on the VM stack above the frame top, under the scratch top (no heap list) | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/mod.rs:409` |
| `IteratorRecord<'s>` | an Iterator Record (§7.4.1): `iterator()`, `next_method()`, `is_done()`; stack-only (R2) because it holds `Local`s | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/iter.rs:30` |
| `PromiseCapability<'s>` | a PromiseCapability Record (§27.2.1.1): `promise`, `resolve`, `reject`; stack-only (R2) because it holds `Local`s | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/jobs.rs:78` |

**ObjectOps v0** (`Cx` methods, spec names): `get`, `get_v`, `set`, `has_property`, `has_own_property`, `get_own_property`, `define_own_property` (ValidateAndApplyPropertyDescriptor), `define_property_or_throw`, `create_data_property(_or_throw)`, `delete`, `delete_property_or_throw`, `own_property_keys` (a JS array: indices ascending, strings in creation order, symbols), `get/set_prototype_of`, `is_extensible`, `prevent_extensions`, `is_callable`, `is_constructor`, `is_array`. Conversions: `to_boolean`, `to_number`, `to_integer_or_infinity`, `to_int32`, `to_uint32`, `to_length`, `to_string`, `to_property_key`, `to_object`, `to_primitive`, `same_value`, `is_strictly_equal`. Spec helpers: `to/from_property_descriptor`, `create_list_from_array_like` (into an `ArgsBuilder`), `get_prototype_from_constructor`, `ordinary_create_from_constructor` (a `prototype` that is not an object falls back to the default intrinsic of GetFunctionRealm(constructor), §10.1.14 step 4). Creation: `new_object(_with_proto)`, `new_array`, `create_array_from_list`, `new_string(_utf16)`, `new_wrapper`, `new_symbol`, `intrinsic(IntrinsicId)`. P3 surface (P2-BTAB): `symbol_for`, `key_for`, `symbol_description`, `descriptive_string`, `this_primitive_value(v, PrimitiveType)`, `set_function_name`, `set_function_length`, `new_bound_function(target, this, args.rest(i))`, `has_intrinsic`, `initial_name(f) -> Option<Local<JsString>>` (the `[[InitialName]]` of a host function, from its FunctionInfo; `None` for bytecode and bound functions; an accessor's functions are named "get x" / "set x"; W4-P3FIX-INITIAL-NAME). Calls: `call`, `call_args(f, this, args.rest(i))`, `call_built`, `construct(f, args, new_target)` with any constructor as new.target, `construct_args`, `construct_built`. **Index access** (ABI-1, `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/index.rs`): `get_index(o, k)`, `has_index(o, k)`, `set_index(o, k, v, throw)` and `delete_index(o, k, throw)` are Get, HasProperty, Set and `[[Delete]]` / DeletePropertyOrThrow with the key `! ToString(𝔽(k))` for a `u64` `k`. A dense element and a missing index need no key, so they make no atom; a stored integer key of an object with at most 64 own properties is found by a scan (the array index from the atom's hash field, or the digits); otherwise the key is interned and the ordinary path runs (`property::get_index` and its neighbours, `crates/ep9_vm/src/property.rs:2077`). A missing array index stops at the receiver while no prototype has an index key (`shape::proto_index_keys`). `poll_interrupt()` takes one interrupt budget unit and polls when it runs out (termination gives an uncatchable `Err(Throw)`): a builtin loop over indices has no back edge, so it calls it once per index. **Iterator protocol** (ABI-3, `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/iter.rs`): `get_iterator(obj)` (GetIterator, sync; the async form comes later as an addition), `get_iterator_from_method(obj, method)`, `iterator_step_value(&mut record) -> Option<Local>` (a throw or done sets `[[Done]]`; one interrupt budget unit per step) and `iterator_close(&record, completion)` (a throw completion keeps its exception whatever `return` does). Accessor properties are built (P3-ACC). v0 limits: these throw "not implemented" until H18 element storage: an accessor on an array index, an array element whose attributes are not {w, e, c}, and a non-writable array `length` (W4-ACC-ELEM).

**Internal slots** (ABI-SLOTS, `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/slots.rs`): `new_object_with_slots(proto, class, n)`, `slot(o, class, i)`, `set_slot(o, class, i, v)` and `object_class(v)`. The class decides what each slot index means, and its builtin module names the indices. Slots hold Values in a heap cell (plan rule 8), and property code never touches them. A slot index past the count panics.

**JIT and AOT** never call a `HostFn` (Rust ABI). They call one `extern "C"` host-call stub `(cx, fp) -> bits` that pushes the host frame and runs `call::call_host`; the builtin is named by its FunctionInfo or its `BuiltinId` (block 7 bits + index 9 bits) through the isolate table or a unit link cell (H35).

### Builtin tables (M0 wk4 P2)

```text
IsolateConfig { builtins: Some(&ep9_builtins::TABLE), .. }
Isolate::new
  builtins::check      both tables pass check_tables (cached per table)  -> IsolateError::BuiltinTable
  Runtime::new
  builtins::prepare    one dense FunctionInfo array + one root range of function slots per module
  realm::init          atoms; core intrinsics: %Object.prototype%, %Function.prototype% (VM builtin 0.0),
                       Array/String/Number/Boolean/Iterator/ArrayIterator/Error prototypes, the global
  builtins::install    VM table (block 0), then the embedder table, modules in order:
                       pass 1 every function object; pass 2 the intrinsics; pass 3 the properties
```

| Type | What it is | Where |
|---|---|---|
| `BuiltinId(u16)` | block (7 bits, < 128) + index (9 bits, < 512): definition `i` of the module with block `b`. Blocks are fixed per module; ids are append-only | `crates/ep9_vm/src/builtins.rs:55` |
| `IntrinsicId` | 72 append-only ids: the core prototypes 0-16 (made by realm init; `SymbolPrototype` by `symbol::install`), the constructors and `%Math%` 17-31 (made by tables), `%StringIteratorPrototype%` 32 (made by the VM table), `%GeneratorFunction%`, its prototype, `%GeneratorPrototype%`, `%AsyncFunction%`, its prototype and `%eval%` 33-38 (the VM table), then `%Promise%`, `%Promise.prototype%`, `%AggregateError%` and `%AggregateError.prototype%` 39-42 (`ep9_builtins` promise.rs, M2-P4), `%Reflect%` 43 (`ep9_builtins` reflect.rs, M2-P5), `%Array.prototype%[@@unscopables]` 44 (the VM table natives.rs, M2-P5 VM-SMALL), and ids 45-63 declared in M3-P0 for the month-3 builders (P0-D3): `%AsyncGeneratorFunction%`, its prototype, `%AsyncGeneratorPrototype%`, `%AsyncIteratorPrototype%`, `%AsyncFromSyncIteratorPrototype%` 45-49 (the VM table, M3-P2 ASYNCGEN); `%Date%`, `%Date.prototype%` 50-51 (`ep9_builtins` date.rs, M3-P1); `%Map%`, `%Set%`, `%WeakMap%`, `%WeakSet%` and their prototypes 52-59, `%MapIteratorPrototype%`, `%SetIteratorPrototype%` 60-61 (collections.rs, M3-P1); `%JSON%` 62 (json.rs, M3-P1, an ordinary object with `%Object.prototype%`); `%ThrowTypeError%` 63 (the VM table, M3-P2 VM-ARGS step 2); and ids 64-71 declared in M4-P0 for the month-4 builders (plan v9 rule 18): `%Proxy%` 64 (`ep9_builtins` proxy.rs, M5-P3 PROXY2), `%RegExp%`, `%RegExp.prototype%`, `%RegExpStringIteratorPrototype%` 65-67 (`ep9_builtins` regexp.rs, M4-P3 REGEXP1), `%WeakRef%`, `%WeakRef.prototype%`, `%FinalizationRegistry%`, `%FinalizationRegistry.prototype%` 68-71 (`ep9_builtins` weakref.rs, M4-P4 WEAKREF). Each maps to a runtime root, except `SymbolPrototype` (kept by `symbol.rs`) and ids 32-71, which are slots 0-39 of the isolate's builtin state (W4-NAT-STRITER-PROTO; `IntrinsicId::slot`) | `crates/ep9_vm/src/builtins.rs:105` |
| `BuiltinDef` | `{name, length, kind: Method \| Getter \| Setter \| Constructor, f: HostFn}`; a getter is named "get x" | `crates/ep9_vm/src/builtins.rs:593` |
| `PropDef` | `{key, value, attrs}`. Key: `Atom` (the `atoms!` list), `Str` (interned at init), `Symbol` (well-known). Value: `Builtin(i)`, `Intrinsic`, `Const`, `Symbol`, `Alias(key)` (the value of a key defined earlier on the same object: one function object under two names), `Accessor {get, set}` | `crates/ep9_vm/src/builtins.rs:819` |
| `BuiltinModule`, `IntrinsicDef`, `ObjectProps` | a module: name, block, `defs`, the intrinsics it makes (`Builtin(i)` or a new ordinary object), the properties per target intrinsic | `crates/ep9_vm/src/builtins.rs:899` |
| `BuiltinTable` | a name and its modules; `check_tables` checks a list of tables | `crates/ep9_vm/src/builtins.rs:978`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/builtins.rs:1024` |

`check_tables` rejects: a block ≥ 128 or used twice; more than 512 definitions; an intrinsic made twice, or a core one made other than as a VM builtin; a property on, or a reference to, an intrinsic nothing makes; a definition index out of range or of the wrong kind (`Builtin` needs a Method or Constructor, `Accessor` a Getter/Setter); an `Alias` of a key not defined earlier on the same object; a key defined twice on one object. It also rejects a table that makes `IntrinsicId::RealmFuses` (id 72; "[[RealmFuses]] is VM-internal: no table makes it"): the realm's fuse block is a FUSE cell (kind 83) that realm init makes (`fuse::new_realm_block`), not an object, and a table's object would replace it in `Root::RealmFuses` (M6-P2 integration fixer, a rewrite of this Frozen core rule, Change log 2026-11-04). A constructor that a table makes must get `prototype` ({} attributes) as its first table key, and must get one (W4-BTAB-CTOR-PROTO-FIRST); the one exception is `%Proxy%`, which has no `prototype` (§28.2.2): `check_tables` takes it with none, and `exotic::is_lazy_prototype` makes none for it (M5-P3 PROXY2, a rewrite of this Frozen core rule, Change log 2026-10-29). Install also refuses, in every build, a key the target already has (a function's own `length`/`name`, or anything realm init made), with `IsolateError::BuiltinTable` (W4-BTAB-INSTALL-NO-SECOND-KEY). Install errors (a well-known symbol or an accessor that does not exist yet) fail `Isolate::new` with a message naming the module and key.

**One function object per builtin and realm.** `builtins::function_object` makes it on first use and keeps it in the module's root slot (the first realm) or in the install list while a later realm installs (`builtins::begin_realm_install`), so an `Alias`, an intrinsic and a property name one object in each realm. Every realm's function object of a definition shares its FunctionInfo. The FunctionInfos sit in one boxed slice per module (no Box per function); `rt.infos` keeps only embedder host functions.

**Ids are golden-checked.** `crates/ep9_vm/tests/golden/vm-builtin-ids.txt` (the VM module plus the `IntrinsicId` list) and `crates/ep9_builtins/tests/golden/ids-<module>.txt` hold one line per definition; a reorder fails them, and `EP9_BLESS=1` rewrites them after a review that sees only appended lines.

**The VM table** (`crates/ep9_vm/src/natives.rs:702`, block 0): `%Function.prototype%` (a function that returns undefined, §20.2.3), `Function` (CreateDynamicFunction, §20.2.1.1, through the compiler hook `compile_function`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/script.rs`; an EvalError on an isolate without hooks), `Object` (new.target, then `to_object`, §20.1.1.1), `Array`, the 7 error constructors, `Object.prototype.toString`/`valueOf` (`toString` gives the builtinTag "Arguments" for an arguments object, mapped or unmapped, §20.1.3.6 step 5; M3-P2 VM-ARGS step 2), `Array.prototype.join`/`toString`/`push` (`push` is generic: a non-Array `this` takes the spec steps, §23.1.3.23), `Array.prototype.values` (also `Array.prototype[@@iterator]`, one function object), `Array.prototype.keys`/`entries` (VM ids 0.28, 0.29; M2-P4 B-ARRAY), `%ArrayIteratorPrototype%.next`, `String.prototype[@@iterator]`, `%StringIteratorPrototype%` with its `next` and `@@toStringTag` "String Iterator", `%Iterator.prototype%[@@iterator]`, the `%Iterator.prototype%[@@toStringTag]` accessor (get "Iterator", set SetterThatIgnoresPrototypeProperties; VM ids 0.31, 0.32), `Function.prototype[@@hasInstance]` (OrdinaryHasInstance; VM id 0.30), the `%Array.prototype%[@@unscopables]` object (`IntrinsicId` 44) (all three M2-P5 VM-SMALL), `Error.prototype.toString`, `%GeneratorFunction%` (through the compiler hook like `Function`), the `%GeneratorPrototype%` methods `next`, `return` and `throw`, `%AsyncFunction%` (CreateDynamicFunction of kind async through the compiler hook, M3-P1 ASYNC; its fallback prototype is `%AsyncFunction.prototype%` of GetFunctionRealm(newTarget), `abi/script.rs`), the async-generator builtins of M3-P2 ASYNCGEN (VM ids 0.33-0.40: `%AsyncGeneratorFunction%`, length 1, through the compiler hook like `Function`, its fallback prototype `%AsyncGeneratorFunction.prototype%` of GetFunctionRealm(newTarget); the `%AsyncGeneratorPrototype%` methods `next`, `return` and `throw`, length 1; `%AsyncIteratorPrototype%[@@asyncIterator]`, length 0; the `%AsyncFromSyncIteratorPrototype%` methods `next`, `return` and `throw`, length 1; with them the VM table makes `IntrinsicId` 45-49), `%ThrowTypeError%` (VM id 0.41, a method with length 0 and name "", `IntrinsicId` 63; M3-P2 VM-ARGS step 2, M3D-2: the `callee` accessor of an unmapped arguments object, §10.4.4.6 step 9, §10.2.4.1; on first use in a realm `crates/ep9_vm/src/arguments.rs` makes its `length` and `name` {w: false, e: false, c: false} and the function non-extensible; a call throws a TypeError), the global `eval` (`%eval%`, indirect eval, §19.2.1; direct eval is `crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/slow/eval.rs`), and the globals `globalThis`, `undefined`, `NaN`, `Infinity`.

**Crate layout.** `ep9_builtins` (engine dependencies ep9_vm and ep9_math only, `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`) has one file per module, each with `BLOCK`, `DEFS`, `INTRINSICS`, `PROPS` and `MODULE`: object 1, function 2, array 3, number 4, boolean 5, string 6, symbol 7, math 8, global 9, promise 10, reflect 11, date 12, collections 13, json 14. `TABLE` lists them in that order, which is the property order. Its `clippy.toml` repeats the root bans (clippy reads only the nearest file) and adds `disallowed-types`: `ep9_value::Value`, `Unpacked`, `Word0` and the ep9_heap cell types (re-exports such as `ep9_vm::Value` resolve to them). `TEMPLATES.md` is the writing guide, and its code blocks are doctests. `ep9_cli` and `tools/test262` pass `TABLE`; test262 `exec` exits 2 when an isolate has no table.

**Embedders get `Local` too** (H20-Q4, filled in M3-P3): `Isolate::with_cx(|cx| ...)` runs a closure with a `Cx`; the `for<'s>` bound keeps every `Local` inside it (a `compile_fail` doctest proves the escape does not compile). `Isolate::with_cx_checkpoint(|cx| ...)` is the same scope, then a microtask checkpoint (also after a throw): a host runs a script or a call in it. The scoped forms of the old `Value` API are `Cx` methods (M0 month 2 P3, C11; `crates/ep9_vm/src/isolate.rs:897`): `run_script(unit, file) -> JsResult<Local>` (loads and runs a unit), `global_binding(name) -> JsResult<Option<Local>>` (GetBindingValue of the global environment: the lexical record first, a binding in its TDZ throws a ReferenceError, then HasProperty and `[[Get]]` on the global object, so a getter runs; `None` = unresolvable) and `display(v) -> String` (runs no JS). The scoped call is the existing `call`, and `global()` keeps its signature. `evaluate_script(source, file) -> JsResult<Local>` (M0 month 2 P3-EVAL-VM, `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/script.rs:65`) compiles a source string as a sloppy Script through the compiler hook `compile_script` and runs it as `run_script` does (`this` = the global object; an EvalError on an isolate without hooks). A `compile_fail` doctest per new form shows that its `Local` cannot leave `with_cx`. `Isolate::with_module_host(resolve, load, f)` (M3-P3 EMBED-MIGRATE) puts the module host hooks in place for `import()` in all the code that runs during `f` (the Modules row). The raw `Isolate::{run_script, call, global, display}` were removed in M3-P3 (EMBED-MIGRATE). `Isolate::heap(&self) -> &Heap` also gave raw `Value`s to safe code (Codex W4-1): `Heap::alloc`, `Heap::find_cell` and `Heap::last_conservative_roots` give `CellPtr`s, and `CellPtr::to_value` makes a `Value` that no scope keeps live. M3-P3 fix round 5 put it in an `impl Isolate` block under `cfg(any(test, feature = "spike-native"))`, so the default build has no `Isolate::heap`; `Isolate::gc_stats(&self) -> GcStats` (a copy of the counters) took its 3 callers outside `ep9_vm` (`ep9_cli --gc-stats` and 2 `ep9_builtins` tests). Now no default-build `Isolate` method takes or gives a raw `Value` or a type whose safe methods give one (`Heap`, `CellPtr`, `ScratchRoots`, `Slot`), except `string_view`, which checks its `Value` (below; H20-Q4). **The embedder entry points are core** (M3-P3 FREEZE-PREP step 2; fix round 5, Codex W4-3; fix round 6, RV5-F3). One test: an entry point is an `Isolate` method that runs ABI code (a `Cx` scope, JS or a `HostFn`), or that takes a hook, a function of the embedder that is called while ABI code runs. The 8 entry points are `with_cx`, `with_cx_checkpoint`, `create_realm`, `perform_microtask_checkpoint`, `take_unhandled_rejections` (the message of a reason that is an object but not an Error is ToString(reason), which can call JS or a `HostFn`; its doctest shows a `HostFn` `toString` that runs in the call), `new` (`IsolateConfig` gives it 3 kinds of hooks: the builtin tables, whose `HostFn`s ABI code calls; the `PromiseRejectionTracker`; and the `CompilerHooks`, which `Cx::evaluate_script` (`abi/script.rs`), `eval` and `Function()` call), `register_host_function` (it takes a `HostFn`) and `with_module_host` (it takes the `resolve` and `load` hooks that `import()` calls; like the compiler hooks, they get no `Cx`). With the path `ep9_vm::Isolate`, they are lines of the core golden (`embed: impl Isolate ::` lines; test `the_golden_has_the_embedder_entry_points`). `Isolate::compiler()` gives the compiler hooks back and takes none, so it is not an entry point. The types that the entry points name are core lines too (fix round 6, RV5-F2; decision M3-P3-H20-CORETYPES): the golden reads them to the end (the type closure), so `Exception`, `SourceLocation`, `IsolateError`, `IsolateConfig` (each pub field, its `Default` impl and `DEFAULT_FEEDBACK_THRESHOLD`), `CompileResult`, `CompileFailure`, `CompilerHooks` and the `Isolate` struct (no pub field) have core `embed:` lines with their public fields and variants, and the pub methods and trait impls (`Display`, `Error`, `Default`) of their impls in the files outside `abi/` and `builtins.rs`. The ABI hook types (`BuiltinTable`, `HostFn`, `PromiseRejectionTracker`) are core lines already. Two kinds of named types are not core: `Dispatch` (the `IsolateConfig::dispatch` field) has `embed (not core):` lines, because it is the list of dispatch expansions of H15 and H15-Q2 is open (`NOT_CORE_TYPES` in the test; decision M3-P3-H20-DISPATCH: `Dispatch` stays embed, not core, as an H15 tuning type); and the types of other crates (`HeapConfig`, `HeapError`, `VerifiedUnit`, `EvalContext`, `FunctionCtorKind`) are not read, because their crates and ADRs own them. Reason: they are the only way from an embedder into the ABI, and the `for<'s>` bound of the `Cx` scopes is the Q4 guarantee; a change to one of them changes what every embedder writes. The other `Isolate` methods (`builtin_table`, `compiler`, `dispatch`, the handles, `collect_garbage`, `verify_heap`, `gc_stats`, `string_view`) are not in the core: they run no ABI code and take no hook (`collect_garbage` runs no finalizer, H12), and they belong to the embedder API of H22, H23 and H19, with those freeze points. The test is for the methods of `Isolate` only, as the code is: the extractor marks `ENTRY_POINTS` only in `impl Isolate` blocks. So `InterruptHandle::request` is not an entry point, though its callback is a function of the embedder that runs at an interrupt poll while JS runs: `request` is a method of `InterruptHandle` (H23-Q6), not of `Isolate`, and `Isolate::interrupt_handle` takes no hook (fix round 7, RV6-A2). `with_module_host` (it takes the `resolve` and `load` hooks) and `new` (it takes the `CompilerHooks` in `IsolateConfig`) are `Isolate` methods, so they still fit the test. With `request` added to `ENTRY_POINTS` (a probe, reverted), the re-blessed golden does not change and `the_golden_has_the_embedder_entry_points` fails. They are still golden lines, marked `embed (not core):` (M3-P3 fix round 2, FP2-GOLDEN-RAW). **The golden is a tripwire** (fix round 7; decision M3-P3-H20-GOLDEN-TRIPWIRE). `h20_core.rs` reads the source text with no compiler data, and a reader of source text cannot list all of a Rust API. So the golden finds a change in the forms that it reads; it is not a complete list of the public API. Since fix round 6 (RV5-F1, RV5-S1; decision M3-P3-H20-FAILCLOSED) it reads more forms. It reads, in every `src` file of the default build: (1) every pub method and const of every inherent `impl Isolate` block, also `impl crate::Isolate` and a block with a `where` clause (the type name is read before `where`); (2) every other impl whose head names `Isolate`, as one line with its associated types, consts and method signatures: a trait impl (`impl Deref for Isolate`, `impl AsRef<Heap> for Isolate`, `impl Drop for Isolate`; none exists today) or an impl that takes it (`impl From<&Isolate> for &Heap`); (3) every free pub fn, const or static outside `abi/` whose line names `Isolate` or a raw type, in any module (today one: `export_facts`); (4) every `pub use` and `pub mod` of `lib.rs` as a `path:` line, and each re-exported item of a crate module and each pub item of a pub module (`versions`, `internals`) as a line; (5) the types that those lines name, to the end (the type closure: the type line, and the pub methods and trait impls of the impls in the files outside `abi/` and `builtins.rs` whose self type is that type), and the types that the core lines name as core lines (above). In the `abi/` files it reads every pub item and the impls whose self type is a pub `abi` type or whose trait is a pub `abi` trait. These forms are `unread:` lines, which fail `h20_core_matches_the_golden` and `the_extractor_reads_every_form`: a type alias of `Isolate` (`type IsoAlias = Isolate`, so an `impl IsoAlias` block), a `use` that renames `Isolate` or a raw type (`use crate::Isolate as Iso`), a type alias that gives a raw type another name, an impl that names `Isolate` inside a fn, block or `macro_rules!` body, a glob re-export and a module file of a pub module. The default build: an item is skipped only if a `cfg` on it, on an inline module around it or on its module in `lib.rs` is false in every default build of every target: `test`, and the features `spike-native` and `ic-stats`, which dep-guard 11 keeps out of the ep9 graph (test `only_the_off_features_are_off` reads the workspace Cargo.toml files: no crate turns them on, and the default ep9_cli build turns on `ep9_vm/jit`). A target `cfg` such as `target_pointer_width = "64"` and every other feature (`jit`, `become`) are read like a target, and the line shows the `cfg` (fix round 3, Codex C2-FP1; fix round 6, RV5-F1 M1). So a new raw producer in one of these forms (for example a raw `Value` form of `Isolate` that comes back) is a new line or an `unread:` line, and a review must bless it. A changed or removed `embed (not core):` line is not an H20 core change. Test `no_embed_line_names_a_raw_type` fails on any line but a `path:` or an `unread:` line (an `unread:` line fails `the_extractor_reads_every_form`) that names `Value`, `Heap`, `CellPtr`, `ScratchRoots` or `Slot` (fix round 5 read only the `Isolate` methods, and before it only `Value`; Codex W4-1), except two exact lines: `string_view`, and `IsolateError`, whose variant `Heap` holds a `HeapError`. The negative controls: `a_new_isolate_method_is_a_new_line` adds a method in `isolate.rs`, one in another file, four under target `cfg`s (a block, an inline module, a module of `lib.rs`) and two that give a `&Heap` and a `CellPtr`, and it checks that the gated `heap` block of `isolate.rs` gives no line; `each_way_to_add_a_raw_producer_fails_the_gate` makes one change at a time: a block under `cfg(feature = "jit")` and under `cfg(feature = "become")` (and no line under `cfg(feature = "ic-stats")`), a `where` clause, a type alias, a renaming `use` and a raw-type alias, `Deref`, `AsRef` and `From` impls, a free fn that `lib.rs` re-exports, a fn in a pub module and a static in a private module, a re-exported fn and an `Isolate` method that give a struct that holds a `&Heap`, a new `Exception` field, an impl in a fn body and a glob re-export. With the verifiers' forms M1-M5 in the real `src` (a probe, reverted), 9 of the 16 `h20_core` tests fail, `h20_core_matches_the_golden` among them. An `Isolate` method with a method-level `cfg` is still a line (the reader skips only gated blocks and modules), which errs on the safe side; and `the_cfg_reader_knows_the_default_build` checks the `cfg` reader on fixed input. The escape classes of the fix-round-5 review (RV5-F1 M1-M5: a block under `cfg(feature = "jit")`, a `where` clause, a type alias of `Isolate`, a trait impl for `Isolate`, a free fn that `lib.rs` re-exports or that a pub module has; RV5-S1) are now lines or `unread:` lines. Known escape classes that the golden does not read (a new raw producer or a changed member of a core type in them gives no line, and every `h20_core` test passes; the fix-round-6 verifiers proved (b)-(e) with probes, and the fix-round-7 verifier proved the RV7-1 form of (e), all reverted): (a) an item that a macro makes outside the core files: the extractor reads the source text without macro expansion and expands only the local `macro_rules!` calls of the core files (Codex C2-FP2, decision M3-P3-H20-GOLDEN-LIMIT; a re-exported one is a line that says so, as `Atom` is); (b) a module with a `#[path]` attribute whose file is not read, and a module file in the same places: a file outside `src/`, not named `.rs`, or whose first name under `src/` is a gated module of `lib.rs` (`tests`, `native`): the directory `src/tests/` and the file `src/native.rs` (RV6-G1, RV7-3; M3-P4 ACCEPT-8 note N2: with `#[cfg(feature = "jit")] #[path = "native.rs"] mod m;` in `lib.rs`, a probe, reverted, the raw producers of `native.rs`, such as `native_target(&Isolate, Value)`, give no line and all 16 tests pass; the same form to a new file fails 9 of them); `sources()` reads every other `.rs` file under `src/`, so a `#[path]` module to such a file is read); (c) in a file outside `abi/` and `builtins.rs`, a trait impl whose head does not name `Isolate` and whose self type is not a type of the closure (in `isolate.rs`, also not `Cx`), for example `impl From<Local<'s>> for Value`, `impl IntoKey<'s> for Value` or `impl From<Value> for Local<'s>` in `string.rs`; and in the `abi/` files and `builtins.rs`, a trait impl whose head does not name `Isolate` and whose trait and self type are not pub `abi` or `builtins.rs` items, such as `impl From<Local<'s>> for Value` or `impl From<Local<'s, FlatStr>> for JsStr<'static>` (RV6-G2, RV6-A1, RV7-2); (d) a type alias, a renaming `use` or an impl in a body for a type of the closure (`Exception`, `IsolateConfig`, ...): the `unread:` rules know only `Isolate` and the raw types (RV6-G3); (e) an impl whose self type is an `abi` type (`Local`, `Cx`, ...) in a file other than the `abi/` files and `builtins.rs` (`impl Cx` of `isolate.rs` is read) (RV6-G4), and an impl of a type of the closure (`Exception`, `IsolateConfig`, ...) in an `abi/` file or `builtins.rs`, unless it is a trait impl of a pub `abi` or `builtins.rs` trait: the type closure reads only the impls of the files outside `abi/` and `builtins.rs`, and the `abi/` reader reads only the impls of pub `abi` and `builtins.rs` types and traits (RV7-1); (f) the types of other crates (`ep9_heap`, `ep9_value`, `ep9_bytecode`; H20-Q4-X1 names the `ep9_heap` case). In every class, an impl whose head names `Isolate` is still a line, in every file (items (1) and (2)): for example `impl crate::Isolate` or `impl From<&crate::Isolate> for crate::isolate::Exception` in `abi/mod.rs` (M3-P4 ACCEPT-8 note N1; probes, reverted: each fails 8 of the 16 tests, and `impl crate::isolate::Exception` with a pub method in `abi/mod.rs` gives no line, class (e)). So the freeze of the core depends on review too: the README §3.1 review record and the M3-P4 ACCEPT-8 H20 rows check each change for the classes (a)-(f) (for (a): each new macro that emits an impl or an item that names `Isolate` or a raw type). A complete check needs compiler data (rustdoc JSON on nightly, or a public-API tool) and is M1 work: H20-Q6. **`Isolate::string_view(&mut self, v: Value)` stays** (product call, M3-P3 FREEZE-PREP step 2), not in the core: (1) it is sound for any `v` (fix round 5, Codex W4-1): it reads `v` only if `Heap::is_cell` says that a live cell of this isolate's heap starts at it (`is_cell` finishes the lazy sweep first) and the cell's kind is a string kind, so a `Value` of a freed cell, of another `ep9_heap::Heap` or of a dropped isolate, or bits that do not start a cell, give `None`; if the memory of a freed cell now holds a new live string, the view shows that string (wrong text, but an in-bounds read of a live string); and the view borrows the isolate exclusively (H19, W4-STR-VIEW-EXCL). Before fix round 5 it had no check: Codex's probes read a freed cell (a `Value` kept in a `Vec` across a GC, zap off), read past a 16-byte cell that `Heap::alloc` made, and ended with SIGSEGV (exit 139) on a `Value` of a dropped isolate and of a dropped embedder heap. Tests: `tests::strings::string_view_reads_only_live_cells_of_its_heap` (in-crate: 64 unrooted strings, a GC with zap on and off, an interior address, an address outside the cage; with the check removed it fails on the first freed string) and the `string_view` doctest with a cell of an embedder-made heap, before and after that heap drops; (2) its two `compile_fail` doctests are the H19 check of the embedder view rule, and a `cfg` gate would make them fail to compile for another reason, so they would pass with no proof; (3) its callers hold raw `Value`s by design: the spike-native SP-Unit check (`spikes/spike-b/src/unit/checks.rs`) and an `ep9_vm` unit test. A scoped no-copy view of a `Local<FlatStr>` can come later as a part-2 addition if an embedder needs one; today `Cx::to_rust_string` copies and `Cx::code_unit_at` reads one unit.

**Public paths** (M3-P3 FREEZE-PREP; closes M2-P1-FIX-NO-REEXPORT and M2-P3-ABI-REEXPORT). Each name of `ep9_vm::abi` has two public paths, `ep9_vm::abi::X` and the crate root `ep9_vm::X`, and both are in the core. The crate root re-exports the whole `abi` list: `IteratorRecord`, `ObjectClass`, `PromiseCapability`, `PromiseRejectionOperation`, `PromiseRejectionTracker` and `PromiseState`, which were only at `ep9_vm::abi`, are now at the root too (no caller changes: `ep9_builtins` uses both forms). The one exception is `ep9_vm::abi::export_facts` (V8-A13): it is not a builtin ABI name and stays at `abi::` only. A name added to `ep9_vm::abi` gets its root re-export in the same change (test `every_abi_name_has_a_crate_root_path`). The table types keep their root paths (`ep9_vm::BuiltinDef` and the others; the `builtins` module is private). `CollectionCursor` (the stack-only cursor of `collection_cursor` and `collection_next`) had no path until M3-P3 FREEZE-PREP step 2: it is `pub` in the private module `abi::collections`. Now `abi` re-exports it (one `pub use` line in `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/mod.rs`, an orchestrator grant), and so does the crate root. The golden has no `path: (none)` line, and `the_golden_covers_the_core` fails if one comes back.

### Freeze table (M2-H20-PARTS)

H20 has two parts (decision M2-H20-PARTS; the table filled by M3-P3 FREEZE-PREP). A Freeze table replaces the single Freeze point (README §3.4):

| Part | What it holds | Freeze point | After the freeze point | Check |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Core | `HostFn`; `Args`, `Rest`, `Local` and its markers (`JsValue`, `JsObject`, `JsFunction`, `JsArray`, `JsString`, `FlatStr`, `JsSymbol`, `JsKey`, the `Downcast` and `From` lists: the golden holds each impl that the `downcast!` and `upcast!` macros make, with its lifetimes, so an upcast keeps its scope `'s`), `Downcast`, `IntoKey`, `PropertyDescriptor`, `ScratchList`, `ArgsBuilder`, `IteratorRecord`, `PromiseCapability`, `CollectionCursor`, `ToPrimitiveHint`, `PrimitiveType`, `PromiseState`, `PromiseRejectionOperation`, `PromiseRejectionTracker`, and the types `ObjectClass` and `IntrinsicId` with their methods; `Cx` and the signature of each public `Cx` method that exists at the freeze (136 at 88998a1, and 136 after M3-P3 EMBED-MIGRATE step 2 at 89fdae1, with the scoped embedder forms `run_script`, `run_module`, `global_binding` and `display`); the embedder entry points of `Isolate` (`with_cx`, `with_cx_checkpoint`, `create_realm`, `perform_microtask_checkpoint`, `take_unhandled_rejections`, `new`, `register_host_function`, `with_module_host`; Decision, "Embedders get `Local` too"), the path `ep9_vm::Isolate`, and the types that the entry points name, to the end, with their public fields, variants, methods and trait impls (`Exception`, `SourceLocation`, `IsolateError`, `IsolateConfig` and its pub fields, `CompileResult`, `CompileFailure`, `CompilerHooks`, the `Isolate` struct; not `Dispatch`, an H15 type, and not the types of other crates; M3-P3 fix round 6, RV5-F2); the table types (`BuiltinId` with its 7 + 9 bit split, `BuiltinDef`, `BuiltinKind`, `PropDef`, `PropKey`, `PropValue`, `PropAttrs` and its constants, `ConstValue`, `IntrinsicDef`, `IntrinsicValue`, `ObjectProps`, `BuiltinModule`, `BuiltinTable`, `WellKnownSymbol`, `check_tables`); the public paths (Decision, "Public paths"); R1-R6 and the other Rules rows | M0 exit | Frozen (README §3.2, "Frozen part"): a changed or removed name, signature, field, variant, derive or path needs an approved amendment with a benchmark or gate output; its kind is rewrite, and G8 counts it after M1 exit | `crates/ep9_vm/tests/h20_core.rs` against `crates/ep9_vm/tests/golden/h20-core.txt` (a changed or removed line fails as a core change): a tripwire for the forms that it reads, not a complete list (Decision, "The golden is a tripwire"); review for the escape classes that it does not read (the README §3.1 review record, the M3-P4 ACCEPT-8 H20 rows); the complete check is H20-Q6 (M1); the Rules checks |
| Part 2 | The append-only lists: the `IntrinsicId` ids (0-72 now), the `BuiltinId`s of each block, the `ObjectClass` ids (0-14 now), the `Cx` method list (a new method), and the method groups in the rows below | None: the lists stay open for appends | Append only. A new id, class or method is a fill (API addition) on an Accepted part (README §3.2, "Accepted" column: orchestrator and review record), not a rewrite, so G8 does not count it. Once added, an entry is held as the core is: its id, place and signature never change | the id goldens (`crates/ep9_vm/tests/golden/vm-builtin-ids.txt`, `crates/ep9_builtins/tests/golden/ids-<module>.txt`), `crates/ep9_vm/tests/golden/object-classes.txt`, and `h20_core` (a new line fails as an addition until it is blessed) |

The rows below are the method groups built in months 2 and 3. The methods that exist at the freeze are core lines of the golden; each group takes additions only.

| Part 2 item | After the freeze | Check |
|---|---|---|
| The `ObjectClass` list (`ep9_vm::abi::ObjectClass`, ids 0-14: ids 7-10 WeakMap, WeakSet, MapIterator, SetIterator appended by M3-P1 B-COLLECTIONS; ids 11-13 WeakRef, FinalizationRegistry, RegExpStringIterator (name "RegExp String Iterator") declared by M4-P0 for M4-P4 WEAKREF and M4-P3 REGEXP1; id 14 RawJson (name "RawJSON", the `[[IsRawJSON]]` slot of `JSON.rawJSON`, 0 slots) appended by M6-P1 SPEC-B) | append-only | golden `crates/ep9_vm/tests/golden/object-classes.txt`; tests `object_classes_match_the_golden`, `swapping_two_object_classes_fails_the_golden` |
| The `Cx` methods `new_object_with_slots`, `slot`, `set_slot`, `object_class` | additions only | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/slots.rs`; `tests::slots` |
| Jobs (M2-P4): the `Cx` methods `enqueue_job(callback, argument)` (HostEnqueuePromiseJob for an embedder job; a TypeError for a non-callable) and `perform_microtask_checkpoint()` (runs every queued job in FIFO order, including jobs queued meanwhile; throws when JS is on the stack, as the spec runs a checkpoint only with an empty execution-context stack; one interrupt budget unit and a poll per job; it stops at the first job that throws and returns that error, leaving the rest queued; termination drops the queue); `Isolate::with_cx_checkpoint` runs one after its scope (also after a throw); `Cx::run_script` and `with_cx` do not; `Isolate::perform_microtask_checkpoint` (both `Isolate` methods are core lines of the golden). [M4-P4, integration fixer A: each time the queue is empty, `perform_microtask_checkpoint` runs ClearKeptObjects (`clear_kept_objects`) and then the FinalizationRegistry cleanup jobs (`cleanup_finalization_registries`); jobs that a callback queues run in the same checkpoint, and a callback that throws stops it like a job that throws. With no WeakRef or FinalizationRegistry both steps do nothing.] [2026-10-27, M4-P4 fixer B, R-GC-P4-3: when a GC during the callbacks found more dead targets and the queue is still empty, the checkpoint runs another ClearKeptObjects and cleanup round, at most 8 in a row (`MAX_CLEANUP_ROUNDS`); the rest waits for the next checkpoint.] [2026-10-27, M4-P4 fixer C, P4-ORCH-8 (Codex C2-1, C2-2, B1-2): replaces the fixer B note. The checkpoint serves cleanup until none is pending, with no round cap; each cleanup callback, JS or builtin, takes one interrupt budget unit and polls (in `cleanup_finalization_registries`), so an interrupt callback runs and a termination stops a callback chain that never ends, and the cells not yet served stay pending; on its `Ok` exit the checkpoint runs ClearKeptObjects once more, after the cleanup and the jobs it queued.] [2026-10-29, M5-P1 integration fixer, from GC5 (a237d2b, 2e207d1): every exit runs it, not only `Ok`: an `Err` exit (a job or a cleanup callback threw, or a poll stopped it) runs ClearKeptObjects too (`checkpoint_exit`), and the queued jobs behind a throw stay queued. The cleanup walks of `weakref.rs` poll every 1,024 cells (`WALK_POLL_CELLS`); a walk that a termination request stops leaves the rest pending, and the checkpoint then ends at once with the termination (`Err`).] | additions only | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/jobs.rs`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/isolate.rs`; `tests::jobs` |
| The rejection tracker (HostPromiseRejectionTracker, §27.2.1.9): `IsolateConfig::promise_rejection_tracker: Option<PromiseRejectionTracker>`, `type PromiseRejectionTracker = for<'s> fn(&mut Cx<'s>, Local<'s, JsObject>, PromiseRejectionOperation)` with `PromiseRejectionOperation { Reject, Handle }`; `Isolate::take_unhandled_rejections() -> Vec<Exception>` (the reasons of promises still rejected without a handler after a checkpoint; the CLI reports the first as `Uncaught <text>`, exit 1; a core entry point because the text of an object reason is ToString, which can run JS or a `HostFn`, and the jobs that this code queues stay queued until the next checkpoint) | additions only | `tests::jobs` |
| Built-in closures: `new_builtin_closure(behaviour: HostFn, name, length, data) -> Local<JsFunction>` (a host function with no `prototype` that is not a constructor; one FunctionInfo per behaviour, name and length per isolate; `data` in the function's context word) and `closure_data(f) -> Local` (undefined for any other function) | additions only | `tests::jobs`; test262 `built-ins/Promise/**/*-function-{name,length,prototype,nonconstructor}.js` |

A getter or setter runs with `receiver` as `this`; a store lands on `receiver` through its `[[DefineOwnProperty]]`, so an array receiver's `length` goes through ArraySetLength (a RangeError for an invalid length). A receiver that is not an object: a store that would create or update a data property on it gives `Ok(false)` and does not throw; a setter found on the chain runs with that receiver as `this` (a sloppy setter sees ToObject of a primitive receiver, or the global object for null or undefined: §10.2.1.2 OrdinaryCallBindThis; a strict setter sees the receiver as it is) and returns `Ok(true)`, or `Err` if it throws (§10.1.9.2) | additions only | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/mod.rs:978`, `:1026`; `ep9_builtins` tests `tests/reflect.rs` (`set_length_on_an_array_receiver`, `set_with_a_primitive_receiver_runs_a_setter`), `tests/vm_small.rs` |
| Realms (M3-P1 REALMS, H22-Q3): the `Cx` methods `create_realm() -> JsResult<Local<JsObject>>` (a new realm with every builtin table; returns its global object; the current realm does not change), `with_realm(global, f) -> JsResult<R>` (runs `f` with that realm current, switches back also on `Err`; a TypeError if `global` is not a realm's global object), `evaluate_script_in(global, source, file) -> JsResult<Local>` (`evaluate_script` in that realm) and `function_realm_intrinsic(f, id) -> Local` (the intrinsic of GetFunctionRealm(f), for spec steps such as ArraySpeciesCreate step 4); and `Isolate::create_realm(f) -> Result<R, Exception>` (the scoped form: a new realm, `f` runs in it) | additions only | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/realm.rs:31`; `ep9_vm` `tests::realm`, `tests::isolate_api::realms_are_made_used_and_dropped`; `ep9_builtins` `tests/realm.rs`; the `Isolate::create_realm` doctest |
| The clock and the time-zone hook (M3-P0 stubs, bodies by M3-P1 B-DATE; C20): `now_epoch_ns(&mut self) -> i128` (nanoseconds since the epoch, `SystemTime` through `ep9_platform::time`; Date divides by 10^6, Temporal.Now can use the nanoseconds) and `local_tz_offset_ms(&mut self, t_ms: f64, is_utc: bool) -> f64` (LocalTZA, §21.4.1.25: `t_ms` is UTC if `is_utc`, else local; a local time in a repeated or a skipped hour gets the offset from before the change; NaN or an infinity gives 0, and so does a finite time so far out that the move to an equivalent year overflows i64, below about -9.2e21 ms, after which TimeClip gives NaN). Later the body of `local_tz_offset_ms` calls the shared time-zone provider, which must sit below `ep9_vm` (today the `tzdb` module of `ep9_temporal`; Date keeps `localtime_r` until a decision row moves it); the signature stays. | Function source text (M6-P2 REALM-CLOSE task 1, for FN-SMALL; plan v11 §4.6, M6-PLAN-A2): the `Cx` method `function_source_text(f) -> JsResult<Option<Local<JsString>>>`: the source text of `f` as a new String, a copy of the source range `source_start..source_end` (UTF-8 byte offsets) of `f`'s compiled function in its unit's decoded source, made on each call (no cache; no new FunctionInfo field: `FunctionInfo::source_text` reads the unit, which lives as long as the isolate). `Ok(None)` for a function whose unit does not embed its source, a range off the UTF-8 character boundaries, a host (built-in or embedder) function, a bound function, a Proxy (asked nothing) and a value that is not a function. Runs no JS; a GC point (the copy); RangeError past the string length limit. For Function.prototype.toString (§20.2.3.5 step 2; M6-P2 SPEC-C). The range is the one the compiler wrote: ep9_frontend writes the `[[SourceText]]` (§10.2) of every function kind (node v26.13.0 prints the same text): a declaration, expression or arrow is its node; a method or accessor starts at its key (the MethodDefinition without `static`, or the object property); a class constructor, the default one too, is its class; the `Function` constructor's function has `anonymous` (BytecodeGen G12, M6-P2 integration fixer). **The HostEnsureCanCompileStrings hook** (M6-P2 REALM-CLOSE task 2; plan v9 V9-A12, plan v11 §4.6): the `Isolate` method `set_compile_strings_hook(&mut self, Option<for<'s> fn(&mut Cx<'s>, &[String], &str, bool) -> JsResult<()>>)` sets the isolate's HostEnsureCanCompileStrings(calleeRealm, parameterStrings, bodyString, direct) (§19.2.1.2); `None` (the default) allows every text. The VM calls it at its one compile point of code from strings (`Cx::compile_with`, `abi/script.rs`), before the compiler hook: for eval of a string (« », the text, direct or not; not for eval of another value) and the `Function` constructor family (the ToString of each parameter, the body, false), never for `Cx::evaluate_script` or `Cx::run_script`. The hook runs with the callee realm current (`%eval%` and `Function` run in their realm, R5; a direct eval in its caller's). `Ok(())` allows; `Err` throws the hook's pending exception, or `EvalError: code generation from strings disallowed for this realm` when none is pending. The hook may run ABI code and JS and collect garbage. Hooks live in a per-thread table keyed by `Runtime::id` (`host::HOOKS`; an isolate is not `Send`, H22-Q5), one entry per isolate; a set drops the entries of dropped isolates. The signature is spelled out, so the embedder needs no type name | additions only | `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/mod.rs` (`function_source_text`), `crates/ep9_vm/src/function_info.rs` (`source_text`); `ep9_vm` `tests::isolate_api` `function_source_text_copies_the_unit_range` (every dispatch, GC stress off and on); `crates/ep9_vm/src/host.rs` (`set_compile_strings_hook`, `ensure_can_compile_strings`), `crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/script.rs` (`compile_with`); `tests::eval` `the_compile_strings_hook_runs_before_each_compile_and_can_refuse`, `the_compile_strings_hook_runs_in_the_callee_realm` (every dispatch, GC stress off and on), `the_compile_strings_hook_is_per_isolate`, and the doctest of `set_compile_strings_hook`; `h20_core` (2 added lines: the `Cx` method and the `embed (not core)` `Isolate` method) |
| Tail calls of host functions (M6-P3 H20Q; H20-Q3; plan v11 §5.3): the `Cx` method `tail_call_args(f, this, args: Rest) -> JsResult<Local>`: PrepareForTailCall, then Call(f, this, args) (§9.4.5, §7.3.14), for a host function whose last step returns that call (`return cx.tail_call_args(f, this, args.rest(k));`). When `f` is a FUNCTION cell and `args` is a tail of the host function's own arguments, it calls nothing: it writes a request into header slots of the host frame (`NEW_TARGET` f, `CONTEXT` this, `BYTECODE_OFFSET` the int32 k + 1; 0, as `push_frame` writes it, is no request) and returns a marker, the hole value. `call::call_host` then pops the host frame and calls `f` in its place: the callee frame starts at the host frame's base and the arguments shift in place, with no list, so a chain of forwards adds no host frame. Any other `f` (a bound function, a proxy, a value that is not callable) or `args` is called at once, as `call_args` does. The marker is only for the host function's own return value: the request is made only when the host function returns it, and a host function that keeps it or passes it on gives the hole value to other code (wrong values, such as `typeof` "object"; no memory fault: a probe with GC stress and the heap verifier). `Function.prototype.call` uses it; `apply` keeps `call_built` (H20-Q3). A bytecode callee still runs in a nested dispatch run (one native level), as from `call_function_object`; a thrown error's stack has no `call` frame, as before and as in node | additions only | `crates/ep9_vm/src/call.rs` (`request_tail`, `host_return`, `host_hole`, `tail_call`, at the end of the file); `call::tail_tests` (`a_tail_call_shifts_the_arguments_in_place`: every dispatch, GC stress off and on, then the heap verifier; `a_tail_call_takes_no_stack_room_for_its_host_frame`); `h20_core` (1 appended line) |

## Rules

| Rule | Check | From |
|---|---|---|
| R1. A `Local` in a Rust local stays live across every call (CSS) | GC-stress tests: `tests::abi`, the test262 GC-stress run | M0 wk4 |
| R2. No `Local`, `Args`, `Rest`, `PropertyDescriptor`, `ArgsBuilder`, `ScratchList`, `IteratorRecord`, `JsStr` or `FlatStr` in Rust heap memory, a static, a thread_local or a boxed closure | `'s` + `!Send` (compiler); `cargo xtask gc-lint` (P2-LINT; its type list is `STACK_ONLY` in `tools/xtask/src/gc_lint.rs`, plus every type that holds one, such as `IteratorRecord`: test `a_vec_of_iterator_records_is_a_finding`); debug on-stack check in `Local::get`, the one read of a `Local` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/local.rs:182`, P2-LINT) | M0 wk4 |
| Named limit H20-Q4-X3 (2026-11-04, M6-P3 H20Q; V9-D28, decision M4-PLAN-D28): R2 is a rule, not a type guarantee, so safe code can keep a `Local`, or a type that holds one, in Rust heap memory inside one `with_cx` scope, and a collection can then free its cell (H20-Q4-X3 and its probe data). For M1 the limit stays, with the R2 checks: S1 does not move objects, so the risk does not change in M1, and a type-level fix (a `Local` that cannot move into heap memory, or a handle scope) is a rewrite of the Frozen core, with a benchmark and a second reviewer (README §3.2). It reopens on a found defect (Reopens if, the first item) | the R2 checks: `cargo xtask gc-lint` (in-tree, and `cargo xtask gc-lint <embedder paths> crates/ep9_vm/src` for an embedder), the debug on-stack check in `Local::get`, the GC-stress runs; no new check | M6-P3 H20Q (V9-D28) |
| R3. Every `&mut Cx` method may allocate and run JS; a view into heap memory borrows `&Cx`, so none lives across one | borrow checker (`code_unit_at`, `string::view`) | M0 wk4 |

A builtin creates objects in its callee's realm | `call::call_host` switches to the host function's realm (`realm::enter_realm_of`, `crates/ep9_vm/src/call.rs:301`) and back after it returns or throws, so `Cx::global`, `Cx::intrinsic`, every new object and every thrown error of a builtin are its own realm's (H20-Q2); tests `ep9_vm` `tests::realm::a_host_function_runs_in_its_realm`, `ep9_builtins` `tests/realm.rs` `values_get_their_realm_prototypes` (a TypeError of `other.Object.defineProperty`, `other.Object.keys`), `ep9_builtins` `tests/realm_r5.rs` (two realms made as `$262.createRealm` makes them; 69 cases over the 14 `ep9_builtins` modules and the VM table, called from the other realm, both ways, directly or through the caller realm's `Reflect.apply`, give the callee realm's objects and errors, and 3 controls give the caller's: 146 lines, equal to node v24.22.1; evidence [M6]), and the `$262.createRealm` test262 files that pass (75 at M3-P1; 107 of the 284 files that name `$262.createRealm` at ec4b841 on aarch64-apple-darwin, M3-P4 ACCEPT-8) [2026-11-04, M6-P3 H20Q; v11in #13, V11-D39, M5-P2-CODEX-R1-SORT regexp2 1: a borrowed `%RegExpStringIteratorPrototype%.next` (realm B's `next` called on an iterator of realm A) runs in B, its own realm, as every builtin does here; its result object is B's, and the match array is A's (A's `exec` makes it). ## Consequences

- **Gains.** A `Local` costs nothing (no handle scope); a host call passes 4 integer words (cx, this, args, new_target), which fits the 4 Win64 argument registers, and returns in one register. Builtins reach the VM only through `Cx`, so `forbid(unsafe_code)` holds in `ep9_builtins`. No `Vec<Local>` is ever needed: lists are a `ScratchList`, a JS array or an `ArgsBuilder`.
- **Costs.** Every builtin spells `<'s>`. R2 is a lint plus a debug check, not a type error. `apply` copies the arguments once; `call` copies none, and its tail call costs about 58 instructions more per call (H20-Q3, 2026-11-04). The LIFO checks run at run time.
- **Costs of the tables.** A block holds at most 512 builtins, and 128 blocks exist; an id never moves, so a removed builtin keeps its slot. A table error shows at `Isolate::new`, not at compile time (the checks are cached per table).
- **Follow-ups.** Done in M0 wk4: gc-lint and the debug check (P2-LINT); the symbol entry points and `%Symbol.prototype%` (P2-SYM merge); accessors in `define_own_property` and `PropValue::Accessor` installs (P3-ACC); the builtins of `TEMPLATES.md` 1-10 and the `@@iterator` protocol (P3-BLTA, P3-BLTB, P3-NAT). Done in M0 month 2 P1: index access (ABI-1) and the iterator protocol (ABI-3). Done in M0 month 3 P1: H22 realms for R5 (REALMS). Done in M0 month 3 P3: the Freeze table, the public paths (with `CollectionCursor`), the two-realm R5 test and the core golden (FREEZE-PREP); the raw `Value` forms of `Isolate` removed (EMBED-MIGRATE) and H20-Q4 filled. Open: H18 element storage for the v0 limits above; the async GetIterator and GetMethod with a well-known symbol (part-2 additions for the builtins that need them). The core freezes at M0 exit (Freeze table).

## Open questions

| ID | Question | Needed by | Proposed default |
|---|---|---|---|
| H20-Q1 | Where does `new.target` live? (= H14-Q1.) v0: the feedback slot of a `[[Construct]]` frame; `Args::new_target` reads it only when the construct bit is set | M1 | A register filled at entry (H14-Q1). **Answered by H14-Q1 (clarify 2026-10-27, M4-P2 ADR-A0; plan v9 §4.1 task 7): not a register.** Since FRAME 2 (2026-10-21) the frame keeps new.target in its header slot fp - 3 (`slot::NEW_TARGET`), and `Args::new_target` reads that slot only when the construct bit is set, else it gives undefined (`crates/ep9_vm/src/abi/mod.rs:153-162`). The "v0" text above is the M0 wk4 state. The signature of `Args::new_target` and every core line stay as they are; no code, test or golden changes |
| H20-Q2 | The realm of a builtin with N realms (with H22-Q3, Q7) | M1 (`$262.createRealm`) | **Filled 2026-10-23 (M3-P1 REALMS): the callee's realm**, the `[[Realm]]` of the host function (`Args::callee`). The VM switches to it before the call (`call::call_host`), so a builtin needs no realm argument: the current realm of its `Cx` is its own. Tests: R5 |
| H20-Q3 | `call`/`apply` without the copy | M1 | One copy now (`call_args`, `ArgsBuilder` into the callee frame); a tail-call host path in M1, as V8's Function.prototype.call shifts the arguments and tail-calls **Filled 2026-11-04 (M6-P3 H20Q; as built): `call` has the tail-call host path, `apply` keeps its one copy.** `Function.prototype.call` returns `cx.tail_call_args(func, thisArg, args.rest(1))` (part-2 row "Tail calls of host functions"): its arguments shift in place into the callee frame, and its host frame takes no stack room while the callee runs. Data (aarch64-apple-darwin, release, `EP9_HASH_SEED=1`, against 39788b53): recursion through `.call` reaches depth 69,901 at the default 64 MiB native stack (40,327 before; direct recursion 80,655); a loop of 2,000,000 `.call`s retires 2,778M instructions (2,662M before: about 58 more per call, the request and the second call entry); BENCH-SET at c7aa0118 (`.github/scripts/perf-ci-v0.mjs`, the instruction medians, two rounds): raytrace +0.09% and +0.18%, deltablue +0.12% and +0.17%, every workload at most +0.24% (limit +0.5%). `apply` keeps CreateListFromArrayLike into an `ArgsBuilder` and one copy into the callee frame, as V8 copies the list to the stack: a tail form of `apply` (the list moved down over the host frame) gave raytrace +0.93% and no deeper recursion (36,155 both), so it was not kept; `Reflect.apply` (not an H20Q file) is the same. Not done (M2): a bytecode callee of a tail call still runs in a nested dispatch run, one native level per `.call`, as from `call_function_object`; a call in the same run needs the interpreter's call path (`crates/ep9_vm/src/interp/slow/call.rs`) |
| H20-Q4 | The embedder API (`Isolate`) handed out raw, unrooted `Value`s: `let v = iso.run_script(..)?; iso.collect_garbage(); iso.display(v)` read freed memory in safe code. Partly settled (wk4 P2): `Isolate::with_cx` is the `Cx` scope, and a `compile_fail` doctest shows a `Local` cannot leave it. M0 month 2 P3 (C11, the add half): the scoped forms `Cx::run_script`, `Cx::global_binding`, `Cx::display` and the existing `Cx::call`, each with a `compile_fail` doctest | M0 exit | **Filled 2026-10-25 (M3-P3; corrected in fix round 5 after Codex W4-1, W4-2, W4-3).** The claim of the first fill ("the hazard is closed" with the four raw forms gone) was false: `Isolate::heap()` still gave safe code raw `Value`s of heap cells (Codex W4-1; below). EMBED-MIGRATE moved every caller to the scoped forms (step 1) and removed `Isolate::{run_script, call, global, display}` (step 2, bfca918; `rg -n 'pub fn (run_script\|call\|global\|display)\(.*(-> Result<Value, Exception>\|-> Option<Value>\|v: Value\))' crates/ep9_vm/src/isolate.rs` prints nothing, and `git grep -l -P '\b(iso\|isolate)\s*\.(run_script\|call\|global\|display)\('` over crates, tools/test262 and spikes finds no caller). A host runs code in `Isolate::with_cx_checkpoint` (a script, a call or a module, then a checkpoint) and gives `import()` its hooks with `Isolate::with_module_host`. Fix round 5 (W4-1) closes the producers that it found through `Isolate` for the default build: (a) `Isolate::heap` is only in the in-crate tests and the spike-native build, so the producers that Codex and the fix-round-5 map found through it (`Heap::alloc` and `alloc_shape`, the `Heap::find_cell` walk, `Heap::last_conservative_roots`, each then `CellPtr::to_value`; the handle and scratch round trips) do not compile in safe default-build code (3 `compile_fail` doctests on `NoRawHeap`; the map's probe file gives 18 E0599 errors, all `no method named heap`); `Isolate::gc_stats` gives the counters; (b) `Isolate::string_view` reads a `Value` only if it starts a live string cell of this isolate's heap (Decision, "Embedders get `Local` too"). Fix round 9 (Codex round 6, RV8-1; e17fca1, 63cf183) closes one more unchecked read, one that needs no `Isolate`, heap or `Local`, so X1-X3 did not cover it: the kind test of `Downcast` (`<JsString as Downcast>::test(bits)`, a public, doc-hidden method) read the cell header of bits that safe code chose, and `test(0x80_0000)` ended with SIGSEGV in a `forbid(unsafe_code)` crate. Now the test is a method of the private `sealed::Sealed` supertrait and takes a `sealed::Token`, which only `ep9_vm` can make (a private field and a `pub(crate)` constructor; `Local::downcast` is the caller). A `T: Downcast` bound still reaches the method, but the caller cannot make the token; two `compile_fail` doctests on `Downcast` hold the direct call (e17fca1, the head of Codex round 7) and the generic route (63cf183, after Codex round 7). The other `pub` items checked and not producers: the `Value` constructors give only immediates (`from_cell_ptr`, `from_bits_unchecked` are `unsafe`), `Local::bits` and `Local::from_bits` are `pub(crate)`, no `pub` `Cx` method takes or gives a `Value`, `HostFn` and `PromiseRejectionTracker` get `Local`s, `Exception` holds `String`s. Three named, reviewed exceptions stay: **H20-Q4-X1 (an embedder heap)**: `ep9_heap` is public, and safe code that names it can make its own `Heap` and get raw `Value`s of its cells (`Heap::new`, `Heap::alloc`, `CellPtr::to_value`). Those cells are not in any isolate's heap, no `ep9_vm` API reads them unchecked (`string_view` gives `None`, also after that heap drops: a doctest, and the map's probe that ended with SIGSEGV before the check), and to close the producer, `CellPtr::to_value` or `Heap::alloc` must become `unsafe`, an API change of `ep9_heap` (H10, H12) and not of H20. **H20-Q4-X2 (tests and spike-native)**: `cfg(test)` and the `spike-native` feature keep `Isolate::heap`, and the spike-native API gives raw `Value`s (`NativeCx::global_value`, the `native.rs` free functions); there `Heap::alloc` can make a string-kind cell with no string layout, and `string_view` reads past it (the map's probe P3: a 16-byte cell with length 10). `spike-native` is a spike feature that dep-guard 11 keeps out of the ep9 graph. **H20-Q4-X3 (a `Local` off the native stack, R2; M3-P3 fix round 6, RV5-S2; decision M3-P3-H20-X3)**: inside one `with_cx` scope, safe code can keep a `Local`, or a type that holds one (`PromiseCapability`, `IteratorRecord`), in Rust heap memory (a `Vec`, a `Box`, a struct that derives `Clone`). The GC does not see it there, so a collection can free its cell, and core `Cx` methods (`string_len`, `to_rust_string`, `code_unit_at`) then read the freed memory; `string_len` has no check, and `code_unit_at` has only a debug check (`debug_assert!(is_string(b))` in `string::units`). Fix round 7 ran the probe again (RV6-A3; a test in `ep9_cli` with `forbid(unsafe_code)`, removed after the run; aarch64-apple-darwin, debug and release, zap on and off; the zap default is on in debug, off in release): (1) 64 strings kept in a `Vec<Local<JsString>>` across a GC, then 64 new strings: with zap on, 3 of 64 `string_len` calls read the zap word and 61 read the bytes of the new strings (`0x5A5A5A5A`, "ZZZZ"), and 0 read the old text; with zap off, 61 read the new bytes and 2 read the old text; debug and release give the same counts, and the debug on-stack check does not fire; (2) 64 `Local<FlatStr>` kept in a `Vec` across two GCs, with no new strings: with zap on, all 64 `string_len` calls read the zap word; then `code_unit_at` stops at the debug `is_string` assert in a debug build (a panic), and in a release build `code_unit_at(f, 0)` gives a byte of the zap word and `code_unit_at(f, 1_000_000_000)` ends with SIGBUS (exit 138); with zap off, in both builds, the first 4 freed cells still give `string_len` 31 (the old length) and `code_unit_at(f, 0)` gives `Some(114)` (the "r" of the old text), and no check fires. R2 forbids this, and R2 is a rule, not a type guarantee: `cargo xtask gc-lint` enforces it only on the in-tree code (`crates/ep9_vm/src` and `crates/ep9_builtins/src`). On an embedder path it knows only the base list `STACK_ONLY` (`Local`, `Args`, `Rest`, `ArgsBuilder`, `ScratchList`, `PropertyDescriptor`, `JsStr`, `FlatStr`); it learns that a type holds a `Local` (`PromiseCapability`, `IteratorRecord`, `CollectionCursor`) only from the files that define it (RV6-A4). So `cargo xtask gc-lint <embedder path>` finds `Vec<Local<'s>>` but not `Vec<PromiseCapability<'s>>`, `Vec<IteratorRecord<'s>>` or `Box<PromiseCapability<'s>>` (fix round 7: 1 finding), and `cargo xtask gc-lint <embedder path> crates/ep9_vm/src` finds all 4, the debug on-stack check in `Local::get` checks only the place of the `Local` that is read, and a `Cx` method takes a `Local` by value, so the copy on the stack passes it (`cx.string_len(v[0])`), and the GC-stress runs find the rest in the tests. Embedders must keep each `Local` on the native stack and can run `cargo xtask gc-lint <their paths> crates/ep9_vm/src` (the `ep9_vm` sources are needed for the types that hold a `Local`). A type-level fix (for example a `Local` that cannot be moved into heap memory, or a handle scope) is an H10/H20 product decision for M1. This hole is older than M3-P3. The embedder entry points (8 since fix round 5, W4-3) and the types that they name (fix round 6, RV5-F2) are core lines of the golden, and every other way to the heap that the golden reads is an `embed (not core):` line (M3-P3 fix round 2; more forms since fix round 6, RV5-F1, RV5-S1; a tripwire, not a complete list, since fix round 7; Decision, "The golden is a tripwire"): the pub methods of the inherent `impl Isolate` blocks (also with a `where` clause), every other impl whose head names `Isolate` (trait impls), every free pub fn, const or static that names `Isolate` or a raw type, every re-export of `lib.rs`, every item of a pub module, and the types that these lines name, under every `cfg` but `test`, `spike-native` and `ic-stats`; an alias of `Isolate` or of a raw type, an impl that names `Isolate` in a body and a glob re-export are `unread:` lines that fail the gate. So a raw form in one of these forms that comes back is a new line or an `unread:` line, and test `no_embed_line_names_a_raw_type` fails on a line that names `Value`, `Heap`, `CellPtr`, `ScratchRoots` or `Slot`. Not read (the Decision lists the classes (a)-(f)): macro-made items outside the core files (M3-P3-H20-GOLDEN-LIMIT), module files (also the files of `#[path]` modules) outside `src/`, not named `.rs`, under `src/tests/`, or `src/native.rs`, trait impls outside `abi/` and `builtins.rs` whose head does not name `Isolate` and whose self type is not a type of the closure (in `isolate.rs`, also not `Cx`), trait impls in `abi/` and `builtins.rs` whose head does not name `Isolate` and whose trait and self type are not pub `abi` or `builtins.rs` items, aliases and renames of the core closure types, impls of `abi` types outside `abi/` and `builtins.rs`, impls of the closure types (`Exception`, `IsolateConfig`, ...) in `abi/` and `builtins.rs` (except a trait impl of a pub `abi` or `builtins.rs` trait, and an impl whose head names `Isolate`), and the types of other crates (X1). For these, the freeze depends on review; H20-Q6 is the complete check (M1). W4-2 (the golden read only the 6 entry-point names) does not hold at 65d9b10: fix round 2 (100e9bb, FP2-GOLDEN-RAW) made the golden read every pub method of every default-build `impl Isolate` block and fix round 3 (28fd8f6) added the `cfg` reader; with `pub fn global(&self, name: &str) -> Option<ep9_value::Value>` added to `isolate.rs` (a probe, reverted), 8 of the 13 `h20_core` tests fail, `h20_core_matches_the_golden` and `no_isolate_method_takes_or_gives_a_raw_value` (renamed `no_embed_line_names_a_raw_type` in fix round 6) among them |
| H20-Q6 | A compiler-derived public-API check (M3-P3 fix round 7, decision M3-P3-H20-GOLDEN-TRIPWIRE). The core golden reads the source text, so it is a tripwire for the forms that it reads (Decision, "The golden is a tripwire"), not a complete list: the escape classes (a)-(f) give no line, and their freeze depends on review. A complete check needs compiler data. Its test list: each RV6 and RV7 form must give a new or changed line: a `#[path]` module to a file under `src/tests/`, outside `src/` or not named `.rs` (RV6-G1, RV7-3); `impl From<Local<'s>> for Value` in `string.rs` and in `abi/local.rs`, and `impl IntoKey<'s> for Value` and `impl From<Value> for Local<'s>` in `string.rs` (RV6-G2, RV7-2), `impl From<Local<'s, FlatStr>> for JsStr<'static>` and `impl<'s, T> From<Local<'s, T>> for Value` in `abi/local.rs` (RV6-A1); `type Ex = Exception; impl Ex`, `use crate::isolate::Exception as Ex2; impl Ex2`, an `impl crate::Exception` in a fn body and an `impl crate::IsolateConfig` in a `const _` body, each with a pub method that gives a `Value` (RV6-G3); `impl Local<'s>` and `impl Cx<'s>` with a pub method in `string.rs` (RV6-G4); `impl crate::isolate::Exception` in `abi/mod.rs` and `impl crate::isolate::IsolateConfig` in `builtins.rs`, each with a pub method that gives a `Value` (RV7-1); and a macro-made item (M3-P3-H20-GOLDEN-LIMIT) | M1 (a new tool or dependency; the Cargo tables are frozen in M3) [2026-11-04, M6-P3 integration fixer; orchestrator decision M6-ORCH-44:] M2: the tool build moves to M2. It is tooling, not ABI; its task line is in the frozen `tools/xtask/src/main.rs`; the decision text stays in this row | A public-API listing from compiler data (rustdoc JSON on nightly, or a public-API tool) of `ep9_vm` in the default build, diffed against a golden; the source-text golden stays as the tripwire until then **Filled 2026-11-04 (M6-P3 H20Q; decision M4-PLAN-D38 kept): the tool is rustdoc JSON on the fuzz nightly `nightly-2026-10-04` (`fuzz/rust-toolchain.toml`; H26-Q3: one nightly pin). It is built in M2, not in M6-P3.** Probe (not kept) at c7aa0118: `cargo +nightly-2026-10-04 rustdoc -p ep9_vm --lib -- -Z unstable-options --output-format json` gives format_version 61, and its pub inherent methods of `Cx` are the 166 `abi: impl<'s> Cx<'s> :: pub fn` lines of the golden, name for name (165 at 39788b53). With the RV6-G4 form added (a pub method in an `impl Cx` block of `string.rs`; reverted), `h20_core` still passes (17 of 17: the escape class), and the rustdoc JSON lists 167 methods, the new one among them. xtask has a JSON reader (`tools/xtask/src/exit/json.rs`), so the tool is one new file, `tools/xtask/src/h20_api.rs`, and no new dependency. Not built in M6-P3: its task line is in the frozen `tools/xtask/src/main.rs`, plan v11 grants the file only with an orchestrator row for that line and none exists, its golden and CI job have no M6-P3 owner, and no code lands after CF. Today `ep9_builtins` json.rs `primitive_data` calls `Cx::this_primitive_value` and catches the TypeError (`Cx::catch_exception`): 10 × `JSON.stringify` of 20,000 small objects takes 0.39 s user, 0.17 s with the probe removed (M3-P1 B-JSON) | M1 (a part-2 append: part 2 has no close point, Freeze table; due date moved from "the part-2 list freeze (M3-P3 FREEZE-PREP)" on 2026-10-25) | A part-2 `Cx` method `primitive_data(v) -> Option<Local>` over `wrapper::wrapper_value` (or an `Option` form of `this_primitive_value`); json.rs `primitive_data` becomes one call **Filled 2026-11-04 (M6-P3 H20Q; the default, built in M5-P3): `Cx::primitive_data(v) -> Option<Local>` (part-2 row "Wrapper slot and proxy tests") reads the slot through `wrapper::wrapper_value`, and json.rs `primitive_data` is one call to it: no TypeError is made or caught.** It reads `[[NumberData]]`, `[[StringData]]` and `[[BooleanData]]`, the slots that JSON reads (SerializeJSONProperty step 4, the replacer list, `space`); a Symbol wrapper gives `None`, and ep9 has no BigInt yet (`[[BigIntData]]` comes with BigInt). Negative control: with Boolean taken out of `Cx::primitive_data` (probe, reverted), both tests fail. ## Reopens if

- A GC-stress failure is traced to a `Local` in Rust heap memory: add a type-aware dylint lint (ABI-5 e).
- The JIT/AOT host-call stub needs more than 4 integer arguments on Win64.

So each isolate of a process has its own sequence, and one `EP9_HASH_SEED` gives the n-th isolate the same sequence each run: the row is true. **Codex round-1 vm4 finding** (row 2), through the same flow: true at 81e8e93 (the seed took no isolate input); sort **(b)**, a defect P0-VM4 made (it added `rng_seed`); FIXED by 2151bed (with a612cfe): `math_random_is_xorshift128_plus_in_0_1` (two isolates differ), `the_rng_seed_is_never_zero` (the isolate's seed is `rng_seed(id)`; a new id gives a new seed) and `one_hash_seed_gives_one_math_random_run` (child processes: `EP9_HASH_SEED=1` twice equal, `2` different) pass. Note (c), no change: the realms of one isolate share its one state (the row says per isolate; Math.random itself is M4-P1 MATH1). The round-2 and r3-docs findings are not on H20. **Tests** (`EP9_HASH_SEED=1`): `h20_core` 16 pass; `tests::layout4` 12 pass; `ep9_builtins --test table` 8 pass. Fixed in cf6e643 (`try_has_index`). Each fix's test fails with the fix taken out (fixer B-2 mutation runs at 0a98596, the code of 94f4568): without the depth check, `a_chain_of_max_forward_depth_proxies_answers_and_one_more_throws` fails in release (10,001 proxies answer); with the depth level given back at once (so the forward can be a tail call again), `the_cycle_check_stops_at_a_proxy_and_the_stack_check_ends_the_loop` runs past 120 s in release (killed), as R-GC-P4-4 saw; without the hop bound, `the_prototype_walks_end_on_a_proxy_cycle` runs past 60 s (killed); with the ordinary `has_index`, `has_index_asks_a_live_an_inherited_and_a_revoked_proxy` fails in debug (the V9-A21 assert) and in release (a wrong answer); without the proxy kinds in `exotic_name`, `named_ics_take_the_generic_path_at_a_proxy` fails (debug). It is on the H20 fixer B row 2 (the B1-1 fix wording, written by fixer C): (b), fixed in 319f303, whose text ('8 units after each full group of 8 steps, none for the last 1 to 8 steps of a walk') is true at HEAD: `instance_ic::hit` walks in groups of `WALK_STEPS` (8): the inner loop returns TRUE (a match), FALSE (the end of the chain) or a miss (a proxy) with no unit, and only after a full group does `*budget -= WALK_STEPS as i64` run, with a miss when the budget is at or below 0. So a walk of 8 steps or fewer takes no unit, the last 1 to 8 steps of a longer walk take none, a walk that uses up a budget of 1 or more runs at most 7 steps past it, and one that starts at 0 or below runs 8 steps (`call_bytecode` in call.rs takes its unit at line 496, after its `poll` at line 470, so a callee can start at 0). A miss goes to the cold path: `property::instance_of` reaches `prototype_walk_step`, which takes a unit and polls at 0 or below at its first step. The docs slice found the cleanup drain true to the code; so did row 1. On this row: no finding. The Change-log row's 'the note above it now reads every exit' means the new note; the fixer C note keeps its text, as a dated note must. (2) Not exact: 'the checkpoint then ends at once with the termination (Err)'. `cleanup_then_poll_a_stopped_walk` forces the poll only after `cleanup_finalization_registries` returns, and that function first serves the cells already moved to CLEARED, polling only at budget 0. So callbacks (JS) can run after the walk saw the termination (the test allows up to 1,023: `tests/weakref.rs:972`, `first < 1024`), and a callback that throws there returns its own exception, not the termination. (1) The change: in the intrinsics loop of `check_tables` (`crates/ep9_vm/src/builtins.rs`), after the prototype-order test and before the core-builtin test, a definition of `IntrinsicId::RealmFuses` (id 72) returns the error '[[RealmFuses]] is VM-internal: no table makes it'. Before 0078fd58 the check accepted such a table. My run, in a detached worktree at 0078fd58^ (436c1483) with only the test hunk of 0078fd58: `tests::fuse::a_table_or_an_embedder_cannot_read_or_replace_the_fuse_block` FAILS at its first step (`unwrap_err()` on an `Ok` value); a probe (not kept) that tries only the table with no prototype fails the same way, so both table forms passed the check. Id 72 is not a core id (`is_core` stops at `GlobalObject`), so the core test did not stop it. At HEAD the test passes. The row's reason holds: `realm::init_core` calls `fuse::new_realm_block` right after the REALM record, and a table intrinsic goes through `set_intrinsic`, which writes its root and the REALM list (`realm::record_intrinsic`), so a table's object would have replaced the FUSE cell in `Root::RealmFuses`. No table in the tree makes id 72 (`git grep RealmFuses crates/ep9_builtins`: none). (2) The other parts of 0078fd58 are as the row says: `function_realm_intrinsic` and `try_function_realm_intrinsic` give undefined for id 72 (a private helper `embedder_intrinsic` in `abi/realm.rs`), and `Cx::intrinsic` panics with 'RealmFuses is VM-internal'. No signature changes, and `git diff 0078fd58^ 0078fd58` changes no golden file (`h20-core.txt` the same). The `runtime.rs` change is the `Root::RealmFuses` doc comment only (3 lines out, 3 in; the rule 2 grant of M6-ORCH-31). I agree that these parts are not part-2 rows and not core rule changes: each gives for id 72 what it gave before FUSE-PI. (3) The line 68 note (a02ceae1) is in-cell (no line moves), names the refusal, its message and its reason, and uses the PROXY2 form ('(..., a rewrite of this Frozen core rule, Change log <date>)'). It matches the code. (4) Kind test (README §3.3 rewrite: 'a check now accepts what it rejected, or rejects what it accepted'): it holds for `check_tables`, a Core item of the H20 Freeze table (Frozen at M0 exit). Author: rewrite (Frozen core: `check_tables`). **Confirmed**: rewrite (Frozen core). README §3.2 'Frozen part' applies: a benchmark, a second reviewer agent from another plan role and a dated decisions-log line. (5) Bucket: the row is dated 2026-11-04; the m1a tag is 2026-11-02 and no m1-exit tag exists, so the rewrite is between M1a and M1 exit: G8 does not count it, it is not pre-freeze churn, and the M1 report lists it by name (README §3.3).
