It wrote none of 0078fd58, none of rows 1-4, no Change-log text and no Codex text. **Accept.** (1) The refusal is needed: no table can make id 72 correctly. A table makes an intrinsic only as a function (`IntrinsicValue::Builtin`) or an ordinary object (`IntrinsicValue::Object`), and id 72 is a FUSE cell (kind 83), not an object; the `IntrinsicId` doc and the part-2 row "The layout export and the golden form, month 6" (M6-P0 VM6) said "no table makes it" before FUSE-PI. No table in the tree makes it (`git grep RealmFuses crates/ep9_builtins`: none). Probe (not committed), with the 6 lines of the refusal removed at HEAD: an embedder table that makes id 72 passed `Isolate::new`; after install `Root::RealmFuses` held an ordinary object (kind 16), not the FUSE cell (kind 83), and `fuse::realm_intact` failed its debug check 'Root::RealmFuses' (left 16, right 83). That check is debug only, so a release build reads a word of the object as the fuse entry, and the pops go to the first FUSE cell, which no root holds now. At HEAD the same table fails `Isolate::new` with 'builtin table module `fuses`: [[RealmFuses]] is VM-internal: no table makes it'. (2) It is the least change to the rule: one test of `d.id` (6 lines) in the intrinsics loop, and the doc comment. No other check, type, `IntrinsicId` method, id or golden line changes. Other forms change more: a core id needs a change to `is_core` (a Core method); a refusal at install moves the error out of `check_tables` and still rejects what was accepted. (3) Every other `check_tables` result stays. The new branch returns only when `d.id == IntrinsicId::RealmFuses`. Mutant (the refusal removed at HEAD): only `tests::fuse::a_table_or_an_embedder_cannot_read_or_replace_the_fuse_block` fails (`unwrap_err()` on an `Ok` value); `tests::builtins` (23 tests) passes with and without the refusal. A reference to id 72 (a property value `Intrinsic(RealmFuses)`) and a property on the target id 72 fail before and after ('[[RealmFuses]] is not made', 'target [[RealmFuses]] is not made'): the `made` set starts with the core ids only, and id 72 is not core, so no table property can name the FUSE cell. The real tables still pass (`ep9_builtins` 472 pass at HEAD, rows 1 and 4). (4) The benchmark shows no cost. I computed it again from the samples file: 31 A (0078fd58^) and 31 B (0078fd58), all exit 0; median A 22,739,960, median B 22,721,228 (-0.08%); the paired median of B - A per round is +13,543 (+0.06%); the quartiles of A and B overlap (the spread is about 75,000, 0.3%). So both signs are noise. The bound: the new test runs once per intrinsic definition (25 in the VM table, at most one per non-core id in the other tables), once per process and table (`OnceLock`), so less than 100 compares in about 22.7 million instructions. The pair measures exactly this change (the PROXY2 condition (i)), and the result is far inside the BENCH-SET limit of plan v11 §5.3 (+0.5%). The two binaries are removed, so the samples file is the only record. (5) The API-side parts are in the right part. `Cx::intrinsic` and `Cx::function_realm_intrinsic` are Core signatures (before M0 exit; the Realms part-2 row names `function_realm_intrinsic`), and `Cx::try_function_realm_intrinsic` is a part-2 method (2026-10-30). 0078fd58 changes no signature and no `h20-core.txt` line. For id 72 each gives the result of the Frozen state: before FUSE-PI the slot was undefined, so both reads gave undefined and `Cx::intrinsic` panicked ('... is not installed'); now they give undefined and a panic ('RealmFuses is VM-internal'). Only FUSE-PI (e759650d) before 0078fd58 gave the FUSE cell out, and no tag and not `main` holds e759650d. A panic text is not an ABI value. So these parts are not part-2 fills and not core rewrites, and the Change-log row is right to name them as context of this rewrite. (6) Kind and bucket: rewrite (Frozen core: `check_tables`), as row 1 confirmed; dated between M1a (2026-11-02) and M1 exit (no tag), so G8 does not count it and the M1 report lists it by name (README §3.3). (1) Q3: `Cx::tail_call_args` is the last method of the `impl Cx` block (`abi/mod.rs`). It calls `call::request_tail`, which makes a request only for a FUNCTION cell, a host top frame and an `args` that is a tail of the arguments of that frame, and writes `NEW_TARGET`, `CONTEXT` and `BYTECODE_OFFSET` (k + 1); else it calls `call_with` at once. `request_tail`, `host_return`, `host_hole`, `tail_call` and `mod tail_tests` are at the end of `call.rs`. `Function.prototype.call` returns `cx.tail_call_args(func, args.get(0), args.rest(1))`, and `apply` keeps `call_built`. (2) Q5: `Cx::primitive_data` reads through `wrapper::wrapper_value` and gives a Number, String or Boolean only; `json.rs` `primitive_data` is one call to it. (3) Q6: `fuzz/rust-toolchain.toml` pins `nightly-2026-10-04`; the golden has 166 `abi: impl<'s> Cx<'s> :: pub fn` lines (165 at 39788b53); `tools/xtask/src/exit/json.rs` exists. The Needed-by cell keeps 'M1' and gets the fixer's dated note (M2; M6-ORCH-44). (4) The #25 note: the golden line `embed (not core): pub const ARRAY_LENGTH: i32` matches `pub const ARRAY_LENGTH: i32 = 3` in the private module `ic::smi` (`ic.rs:149`).
